### Page 2

2  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/
上週重要市場資訊
⚫ 歐洲央行上周四(9/10)為年內第二次上調利率，以應對能源價格上漲帶來的通膨
壓力 ， 並警告物價壓力可能比先前預期更久 ， 推動市場加大對後續進一步收緊政
策的押注 。 歐洲央行總裁拉加德形容此次决定爲”無需多想的决定”， 並警告通膨
回落至 2%目標的時間點目前預計在 2027 年底。歐洲央行預期明年平均通膨爲
2.5%，高於 6 月的 2.3%；2027 年核心通膨預期爲 2.6%，高於此前的 2.5%。
⚫ 軍事消息人士稱，與伊朗結盟的胡塞武裝上周四(9/10)奪取葉門港口城市的控制
權 ， 並沿紅海海岸向南推進至戰略島嶼地區 。 美國總統川普表示 ， 他預計伊朗戰
爭將在美國中期選舉後結束。若胡塞武裝控制這條與 荷姆茲海峽同樣重要的水
道 ， 其支持者伊朗將在與美國的戰爭中獲得關鍵戰略優勢 ， 並進一步推高油價 。
⚫ 美國勞工部勞工統計局上週四(9/10)公布 8 月生産者物價指數 PPI 年增 5.4%，

高於市場預期的 5.3％。週五(9/11)公布 8 月消費者物價指數(CPI)年增 3.4%與前
月相同，核心 CPI 年增率 2.4%符合市場預期，月增率達 0.3%高於市場預期。
FED WATCH顯示數據公布後推升市場對於聯準會本周將升息的預期。
⚫ 日本央行數據顯示，8 月國內企業物價指數較去年同期上漲 7.6%。增幅高於市
場預估中值 7.4%。7 月漲幅經修正後爲 7.7%；8 月進口物價指數同比上漲 24.8%，
7 月修正後爲上漲 29.3%， 表明當月日圓貶值以及中東局勢影響推高進口成本和
整體通膨，同時鞏固市場對日本央行在 9 月會議進一步升息預期。

### Page 3

3  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/
美元兌新台幣匯率走勢
Sep/14/2026
＜上週走勢＞
上週台幣匯價高點為 31.455，低點在 31.698，以 31.638 作收，較前一週收盤價
貶值 0.8 分。
上週台幣先升後貶，週初，受到市場對於日本央行 9 月升息的預期升溫，日圓
走升帶動美元指數跌破 99 整數價位低檔震盪 ， 加以台股高檔震盪 ， 外資延續匯入的
走勢，帶動台幣升破31.500 至 31.455 週內高點；臨近週末，美國公布8 月生產者物
價指數年增率由上個月的 4.8%上升至 5.4%，數據公布後，FEDWATCH顯示聯準會
9 月升息機率攀升至 7 成，美元指數回到 99 上方，台股高點回落，外資偏向匯出，
台幣由升轉貶至 31.698 週內低點，終場在央行調節之下收在 31.638。
＜未來分析＞
預估本週台幣區間震盪。國際市場方面，上週五公布美國 8 月核心消費者物價
指數月增率 0.3%高於市場預期的 0.2%，數據公布後，提高本週聯準會升息的機率

至接近 9 成，此外中東局勢仍有升溫風險，油價持續高檔震盪，美元指數在 99 整數
關口盤整；就台灣市場來看，台灣 8 月出口衝上 824 億美元，創下歷年單月新高紀
錄，月增 9.4%，年增 41%，展現極為強勁的成長動能。顯示受惠於 AI 需求全面爆
發，帶動外銷出口暢旺，基本面強勁持續支撐台幣的表現；盤勢上，匯價逢低吸引
進口商及政府基金買盤進場，逢高則面臨出口商賣美金，且本週台美均有利率決策
會議，預估匯率波動將加劇。綜合以上，預估本週台幣走勢區間震盪。
＜預估本週＞ 美元兌台幣：31.400-31.900

### Page 4

4  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/ 
美元兌人民幣匯率走勢  
Sep/14/2026 
＜上週走勢＞ 
    上週人民幣震盪走升。週初，雖然美伊戰火未平，帶動油價及美債利率高漲，
但市場對於日本央行 9 月升息預期升溫，日圓偏升帶動美元指數跌破 99 價位後低
檔震盪，人民幣延續偏升走勢，走升至6.7022 週內高點；臨近週末，美國公布8 月
生產者物價指數 5.4%優於市場預期 5.3%， 加以8 月核心消費者物價指數月增率 0.3%
優於市場預期的 0.2%，數據公布後，FEDWATCH顯示本週聯準會 9 月升息預期大
幅攀升至近 9 成，美元指數回到99 上方，人民幣回吐部分週初以來的漲幅，終場境
外人民幣收在 6.7071，週線升值 8 點，境內人民幣則收在6.7079，週線升值 29 點。  
＜未來分析＞ 
     預估本週人民幣區間偏升。國際市場方面，美國通膨數據表現優於市場預期，
加以油價攀升至每桶 100 美元上方 ， 美債殖利率位居高檔 ， 美元指數也在99 上方盤
整，關注本週聯準會最新利率決議，預計在會議結果出爐以前，聯準會升息預期將
支撐美元指數，限制人民幣升值的空間。中國國內方面，人行中間價格緩步偏升，
上週升破 6.7800 價位，持續釋放人民幣偏強信號，支撐人民幣延續升值的方向。技
術面方面 ，USD/CNH 匯價居於各天期均線下方 ，且均線向下發散 ，加以匯價由低點
向上反彈測試 6.7150 關鍵價位後面臨壓力，顯示空方動能較盛，預計人民幣將延續
升值的態勢。綜合以上，預估本週人民幣走勢區間偏升。

＜預估本週＞ 美元兌人民幣：6.6800-6.7300

### Page 5

5  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/ 
美元兌日圓匯率走勢  
         Sep/14/2026

＜上週走勢＞ 
    上週 USD/JPY 震盪下跌 。週初 ，因市場預期日本央行將加快升息步伐及資金將
回流日本，使 USD/JPY 跌破至 155 整數關卡之下；週間，USD/JPY 延續跌勢最低
來到 152.87 後反彈，後續大致在 153 至 154 間波動；臨近週末，美國公布 8 月 PPI
及 CPI 數據顯示通膨仍處高位 ，USD/JPY 一度反彈至154.6 後回落 ， 終場收在153.54，
週線大跌 270 點。 
＜未來分析＞ 
  預估本週 USD/JPY 區間震盪，主要原因如下：一、上週公布日本第二季 GDP
較上季增長 0.4%，季增年率也由 1.1%上升至 1.4%，主因為企業資本支出改善，而
7 月名目薪資年增 4.7%為 30 年來最大漲幅，而實質薪資年增 2.4%也為 5 年多來最
大漲幅，顯示日本經濟復甦力道較原先預期強勁，進一步強化市場對於日央升息的
預期，有利日圓表現。二、美國 8 月 CPI 及 PPI 數據顯示美國通膨仍處高位，強化
市場對聯準會升息預期，且中東局勢升溫，帶動油價突破 100 美元，預期將支撐美
元，限縮 USD/JPY 下行空間。三、就技術面而言，日線圖中，USD/JPY 匯價多次向
下測試 153 整數關卡未能有效突破，顯示下方有買盤支撐，而 155 關卡附近則為前
波壓力位，因此短線先以區間看待。綜合以上，預估USD/JPY 本週呈現區間震盪的
可能性較高。 
＜預估本週＞ 美元兌日幣：152.00-157.00 
前週交易區間 
152.87 156.27

### Page 6

6  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/ 
美元兌歐元匯率走勢  
         Sep/14/2026

＜上週走勢＞ 
    上週 EUR/USD 小幅下跌。 週初 ，EUR/USD 大致在 1.1600 至 1.1635 窄幅波動；
週間，市場等待歐洲央行利率決議及 美國通膨數據，EUR/USD 持續在 1.1615 至
1.1653 波動；週四晚間公布歐洲央行利率決議如預期升息 1 碼，而美國 8 月 PPI 高
於預期使市場對聯準會本月升息預期上升，EUR/USD 下行跌破 1.16 整數關口；臨
近週末，美國公布 8 月 CPI 顯示通膨仍具韌性，帶動 EUR/USD 走低來至 1.1568，
終場收在 1.1598，週線下跌 15 點。 
＜未來分析＞ 
  預估本週 EUR/USD 區間震盪，主要原因如下：一、上週歐洲央行公布利率決
議如預期升息一碼，總裁拉加德表示即使面對能源價格衝擊，歐洲經濟第二季依然
展現韌性，預期第三季仍會延續，但未來通膨仍面臨上行風險，偏鷹派言論使市場
對於歐洲央行未來持續升息的預期上升，將帶給歐元支撐。二、美國 8 月 PPI 月增
0.4%符合市場預期 ， 年增5.4%高於預期 ，而 8 月 CPI 月增 0.4%年增 3.4%符合預期 ，
顯示通膨仍維持高位，使市場對聯準會升息預期增加，將支撐美元，不利歐元匯價
上行。三、技術面而言，在日線圖中，EUR/USD 匯價雖一度突破 1.1630 年線但未
能站穩，顯示上方仍有賣壓，而下方1.1550 附近則有支撐，在有效突破前以區間震
盪看待。綜合以上，預估 EUR/USD 本週呈現區間震盪的可能性較高。 
＜預估本週＞ 歐元兌美元：1.1530-1.1680 
前週交易區間 
1.1568 1.1653

7  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/ 
                         新台幣利率走勢分析

115.09.07-115.09.11 
銀行隔夜拆款利率 0.805% ~ 0.830%  
30 天期短票利率   1.66% ~ 1.69% 
公債－10 年期-A15106 1.92 % ~ 1.921% 
－ 5 年期- A15108 1.822% ~ 1.823%

一、 台幣貨幣市場部分 ，本週央行連日減發定存單 ， 使市場資金呈現平穩態勢 ， 且
資金需求不若先前，銀行間交投較為清淡，令短率持穩於近期區間。 
週 五 隔 夜 拆 款 利 率 區 間 在 0.805%~0.830% ， 30 天 期 短 票 主 要 成 交 在
1.67%~1.69%，利率較上週上升 1 個基本點。 
二、 本週 5 年期指標券 A15108 成交在約 1.82%，10 年期指標券 A15106 成交在約
1.92%。 
美伊衝突升溫，帶動國際油價持續走揚，加劇市場對通膨之疑慮，加上美國8
月非農就業人數大增、8 月 PPI 年增率高於市場預期，市場預估聯準會下週升
息機率升至七成，帶動美債利率大幅反彈；台灣央行將於下週召開理監事會
議 ， 在台灣經濟成長與通膨維持高位下 ， 台灣央行面臨緊縮壓力 ， 預期台債殖
利率面臨走升壓力 ， 預估5 年券區間為 1.83%~1.90%，10 年券區間為 1.93%~2%。

產品組合

股價相對大盤走勢

南亞科 (2408 TT)  Buy

獲利處於上行，建議 Buy。  
投資建議 ： AI 運算 (GPU 、 TPU、 CPU 及 ASIC) 主導整體 DRAM
需求強勁成長，全球記憶體產業景氣循環邁入長期結構性擴張。
AI 應用領域持續擴大，推升更高容量、更高頻寬及客製化記憶
體架構需求，需求已由核心之 HBM 與 LPDDR5 全面擴展至網
路、儲存及系統管理等應用，而供應商將產能部署於 HBM 與先
進製程，對常規 DRAM 造成實質產能排擠。南亞科目前簽署長
期供應協議 (LTA) 的產能約占總出貨量的 50~70% 。不過南亞科
位元出貨量已達高點，現有廠區在 4Q27 新廠量產前產能維持
滿載，出貨量維持平穩走勢，加上公司庫存處於低檔，預估南
亞科 2027 年 bit shipment 持平，預估南亞科 2027 年稅後 EPS 
127.64 元。主要 DRAM 大廠將產能重心轉向 HBM 與 DDR5 等
高階產品，壓縮 DDR4 供給，使南亞科逐步成為 DDR4 的最大
供應商，議價能力持續提升，建議南亞科 Buy，目標價 635 元。 
3Q26 稅後 EPS 25.40 元：受惠 AI 基礎設施拉貨與長約效益浮
現，3Q26 營收為 1,336.49 億元，QoQ+61.90% ，ASP QoQ>+60% 。
匯率對營收影響 QoQ+1~3% ， bit shipment QoQ-4~-6% 。毛利率
受 Blend ASP 上升， 3Q26 毛利率上揚至 85.66% ，增加 6.17 個
百分點。南亞科 3Q26 稅後 EPS 25.40 元，淨值 118.99 元。  
預計 2026 年資本支出 697 億元 ：南亞科董事會決議為加速 5A
新廠建置並提前支付設備預付款，將 2026 年資本支出預算由原
訂的新台幣 520 億元，大幅上調至不超過新台幣 697 億元，增
加 177 億元，增幅約 34% ，較 2025 年資本支出新台幣 134 億
元，YoY 增加 420.15% ，資本支出調升主要係於 5A 新廠建置與
EUV 先進設備預付款。資本支出中 50% 用在新廠的廠務和無塵
室等，其餘 50% 則會使用在設備採購上。 5A 新廠第一階段月產
能規劃為 35.9K 片，預計於 1Q27 進行設備移入裝機， 4Q27 開
始投產貢獻出貨，目標於 2028 年底前達成每月投片量 30K 片，
並於 2030 年底前達成全產能每月投片量 50K 片之長期目標。

2 群益投顧 
  南亞科 ESG ：  
佈局綠色科技生產  
DRAM 為智慧世代的關鍵零組件，南亞科研發先進且具高效能的 「環境友善
產品」以符合市場與客戶需求。所有產品於設計初期時，即導入生命週期思
維與綠色設計，透過綠色產品推動委員會進行綠色產品管理。

重視人才發展與培養  
「人才」是公司創新、營運與永續發展的根本，強化公司全方位人才培育與
發展，南亞科技與長庚大學簽署成立記憶體學程碩士班，學生除可獲高額獎
學金，更可獲得畢業後的就業機會。南亞科技期盼從育才、用才、引才、聚
才、留才各方面，強化臺灣記憶體產業在國際上的競爭力。

共同攜手永續夥伴  
南亞科攜手上下游合作夥伴一同前行， 295 家供應商認同公司的永續理念，
一同簽署南亞科技企業社會責任承諾書且簽署率達 100% 、輔導供應商進行
永續改善，稽核、輔導與改善率均 100% 完成，並持續辦理供應鏈研討會。
此外，也與供應商共同合作推動人權及環境相關的議題。

永續績效備受肯定  
南亞科視 ESG 為企業的重要承諾，南亞科追求經營績效的同時，也同步落
實 ESG 的平衡發展。南亞科技參加 2025 第 18 屆台灣企業永續獎評選，公
司已連續第九年獲頒多項殊榮，獲獎包括年度最佳報告 (製造業 )、台灣 100
大永續典範企業獎及四項永續單項績效  – 氣候領袖獎、創新成長領袖獎、
資訊安全領袖獎與透明誠信領袖獎。  
南亞科已連續第五年入選 DJSI 世界指數，並連續第七年入選 S&P 全球永續
年鑑，持續獲得國際永續評鑑機構肯定。  
ASP 大幅增加，南亞科 3Q26 稅後 EPS 25.40 元，淨值 118.99 元：  
受惠長期合約 (LTA) 重新簽訂帶動 ASP 跳升與 AI 運算拉貨動能增溫，南亞
科 3Q26 營業收入達新台幣 1,336.49 億元， QoQ+61.90% ， YoY+611.70% 。
受長期合約重新議價影響，平均銷售單價 (ASP)QoQ>+60% ，YoY>500% ，bit 
shipment( 位元出貨量 )則因產能受限而 QoQ-4~-6% ，匯率變動則產生 1~3%
之有利影響。  
長約到期的重新簽約為近期單價上漲的主要動力 。 3Q26 平均售價 (ASP) 季增
超過 60% ，其中超過一半的漲幅來自於半年前至一年前簽訂的高涵蓋率長
約到期並重新定價。  
終端應用結構方面， AI 基礎設施 (含伺服器 )營收貢獻顯著攀升，佔比已正式
突破 20%，其餘出貨則涵蓋儲存 SSD、網通、車用工控與消費性電子等多元

將 2026 年資本支出預算由原訂的新台幣 520 億元，大幅上調至不超過新台
幣 697 億元，增加 177 億元，增幅約 34% ，較 2025 年資本支出新台幣 134

舊費用將較 2025 年減少 10% 左右，等到新廠 2027 年量產後，折舊費用才

4 群益投顧 
5A 新廠第一階段月產能規劃為 35.9K 片，預計於 1Q27 進行設備移入裝機，
4Q27 開始投產貢獻出貨，目標於 2028 年底前達成每月投片量 30K 片，並
於 2030 年底前達成全產能每月投片量 50K 片之長期目標。  
此外，公司已規劃於屏東科學園區建置 3D 晶圓封裝與測試廠，全面支援先
進記憶體架構封裝需求。屏東廠長期總資本支出估計高達 100 億美元，預計
於 2H28 開始進行設備移入與裝機。南亞科 (前段晶圓級 )主導前段的晶圓級
處理 (Wafer-level processing) 與晶圓級測試 (Wafer-level testing) 。由合作夥伴
福懋科負責後續的晶圓研磨、切割、打線、晶片封裝與最終測試。  
受惠技術自主研發與客製化合作深化，南亞科藉由靈活之產能配置積極應
對市場規格演進。產品布局上維持高度多樣化策略，同時供貨 DDR5 、
LPDDR5/5X 、DDR4、LPDDR4/4X 及 DDR3 系列，針對常規應用與利基型規
格動態調配投片比例，並透過 LTA 長約機制鎖定產能與合理利潤，有效抵
禦成熟規格現貨報價波動之潛在風險。  
1B/1C/1D 和 EUV 進度  
南亞科的 10nm 製程分為 A、 B、 C、 D、 E 世代，其記憶儲存細胞 (Cell) 可進
行微縮，因此可由 1A 逐步推進至 1B 再到 1C。製程轉換照計劃進行，會視
整體景氣動態調整。 1B 製程已滿載且良率高， 2026 年產出目標占比達 40%。
1C 製程預計於 1Q27 正式交付客戶進行驗證，次世代 1D、1E 製程及極紫外
光 (EUV) 微影技術導入開發進度亦完全符合內部規劃。現有廠區在既有空間
內持續優化先進節點投片轉換，以確保 DDR5 與 LPDDR5 等高階規格產出
持續攀升；  
EUV 在 1C 和 1D 製程都有導入，甚至 1E，還是要看客戶需求，目前預期
2028 年導入採用；在興建新廠時亦會同步建置 EUV 極紫外光微影設備獨立
廠房。 1C/1D/1E 及 EUV 研發也依計畫推進。  
4Q26 成長來自價格向上，預估南亞科 4Q26 稅後 EPS 28.94 元：  
DRAM 是算力的關鍵零組件，在 AI 驅動的投資下，推動記憶體供需結構性
改變，降低景氣循環因素。 HBM、LPDDR5 與 RDIMM 需求強勁。 HBM、高
容量 DDR5 及 LPDDR5 是整體 DRAM 需求成長的主要動能，高階 e-SSD 、
BMC 、 NIC 對 DDR4 、 LPDDR4 及 DDR5 需求持續。  
一般型 DRAM 方面，高階 PC 與手機對 DDR5 及 LPDDR5 需求維持穩定；
其他消費性方面，高價消費型產品需求暢旺，低價產品需求放緩，整體而言，
AI 需求強排擠手機、 PC、車用及消費型記憶體供給，預計記憶體供給不足
預期將持續數季以上，長期 LTA ( 數年合約 )形成供需共識。  
AI 伺服器與高效能運算拉貨動能外溢，加上手機與一般運算記憶體供給端
受限， 4Q26 DRAM 產業結構性缺貨態勢維持不變。原廠庫存普遍偏低，且
供應商均依據 LTA 長約承諾進行排產，新廠建置、設備交期與新製程導入
均需要較長前置時間，整體供給彈性極為有限，供需緊俏格局為 4Q26 營運
提供基本面支撐。 4Q26 報價已確定高於 3Q26 ，南亞科多數產品合約價持續
上揚，公司對整體價格走勢樂觀，且長約占比持續提高，短單價格仍可能因
市況波動而調整。南亞科 2H26 成長主要來自 ASP，因為庫存在低點，產能
利用率已滿載，預估南亞科 4Q26 稅後 EPS 28.94 元。預估 2026 年稅後 EPS

### Page 5

個股報告

5 群益投顧 
76.44 元。  
產能受限，預估南亞科 2027 年稅後 EPS 127.64 元：  
AI 運算 (GPU 、 TPU、 CPU 及 ASIC) 主導整體 DRAM 需求強勁成長，全球記
憶體產業景氣循環邁入長期結構性擴張。 AI 應用領域持續擴大，推升更高
容量 、 更高頻寬及客製化記憶體架構需求 ， 需求已由核心之HBM 與 LPDDR5
全面擴展至網路、儲存及系統管理等應用，而供應商將產能部署於 HBM 與
先進製程，對常規 DRAM 造成實質產能排擠，使 DRAM 供需缺口將長期持
續 ， 關鍵觀察指標為北美CSP 及大型語言模型開發商的 AI 投資回報率 (ROI)
是否可持續。  
傳統應用市場在供給不足與價格調漲環境下，傳統應用產品出現規格下調
(down-specification) 或延長生命週期的狀況，低階市場部分由二手或回收
DRAM 支撐。  
DDR4 走勢：  
 供給短缺：由於主要供應商減產並將產能轉移至  HBM/DDR5 ，DDR4 市
場出現供給短缺，且此狀況預計將持續數個季度。  
 市場轉移至 DDR5 ：雖然 DDR4 市場占比逐漸變小，目前單一 DDR4 產
品仍佔整體 DRAM 市場總需求 12%~14% ， 若加計 LPDDR4 則比例更高 。
南亞科全球 DRAM 市佔率約為 3%，因此相對龐大的 DDR4 需求，為公
司提供相當充沛且健康的銷售空間。  
 需求穩固：消費性電子是 DDR4 需求最穩固的領域。 8Gb 容量是目前市
場的最佳選擇點 (sweet spot) 。每個客戶的應用中，可能有完整的基礎設
施 (平台 )，包括 PCB、晶片組等等。所以，短期對客戶來說，從 DDR4 轉
移到  DDR5 相對複雜，客戶必須在基礎設施變更上花費大量的時間和金
錢，且 16Gb DDR5 價格也上漲，成本高昂，因此需求將維持較長時間。
 南亞科 16Gb DDR4 於 2H26 開始貢獻營收。南亞科具備高度彈性，可隨
客戶需求與市場效益，在 DDR4 、 LPDDR4 、 DDR5 與 AI 專案之間彈性
調配產能。   
DDR5 ：  
 AI 運算 (GPU、TPU、CPU、ASIC) 全面驅動高容量與高頻寬記憶體需求，
加上個人電腦 (PC) 領域主要採用 DDR5 ，帶動 DDR5 需求穩健成長。  
 南亞科進度：公司已開始以模組形式出貨 DDR5 ，技術進度與市場同步，
高階的 6400 Mbps 產品已準備就緒。  
 目前 DDR5 交易量約占 10% ，公司目標是維持在 10% 以上並逐步提升，
但仍需視整體缺貨結構而定，短期內不會過度拉升產出，以避免對客戶
造成調整壓力。 DDR5 8Gb R-DIMM 主要應用於雲端與伺服器，但在既
有產能未擴增的情況下，大量出貨仍需等待新廠投產後才有空間。

Wafer-on-Wafer (WoW) 堆疊技術：

### Page 6

個股報告  
                                        2026 年 10 月 13 日

6 群益投顧 
 公司正積極開發  WoW 技術，以應對未來  AI/AGI 應用所需的高頻寬與
高密度需求。  
 目前相關設備已安裝，並正與多家客戶合作開發不同領域的終端產品，
但因保密協議無法透露具體應用。預估 2027 年有營收貢獻。  
 南亞科正與多位客戶合作開發客製化  DRAM ，其中也包含  HBM 的延
伸產品以及  Wafer-to-Wafer 封裝專案。由於具體進度取決於客戶端的
市場開發時程，目前尚無法給出明確的營收貢獻指引  
 台灣擁有領先晶圓代工廠 (如台積電 )，這的確有助於簡化並推動南亞科
在客製化專案 (如  HBM 及  Wafer-to-Wafer) 上的進行。  
南亞科的長期合約 (LTA) 佔總出貨量的涵蓋率約落在 50%~70% 。 合約期限從
單季到 3 年不等，其中 3 年期的超長約已佔出貨量  20% 以上。隨著 AI 與伺
服器客戶積極爭取長期產能保障，南亞科正持續與客戶推進更長天期的供
應合約，計畫逐步將過去短天期的 1 年期合約，往 3 年以上的「長期供應合
約」進行結構性轉換，以鎖定長期合作夥伴的份額。為應對記憶體產業的週
期性波動，南亞科正與客戶洽談導入包含 「價格底線與上限」 的合約機制 (如
保證採購、價格底線或預付款 )，在保障供應商合理利潤的同時，也為客戶
鎖定成本上限。這些長期合約不僅涵蓋現有的廠房產能，也涵蓋未來新廠的
整體產能規劃。一般來說，如果是季度合約，價格會在一個季度內維持固定，
雙方都會遵守約定，並在下一次談判週期到來前盡量不作調整。  
產業結構性轉變擴大需求缺口，加速多元創新  AI 解決方案，記憶體需求
維持熱絡， AI 相關需求降低景氣循環波動， AI GPU /CPU/ASIC 晶片開發持
續推升 HBM 、 LPDDR5 及 DDR5 需求大幅成長。 AI 基礎建設帶動  eSSD 、
SmartNIC 與 BMC 採用更高容量、更高效 DRAM ，以因應高速資料處理、網
路傳輸及系統管理需求。非 AI 應用產能受限，有利於高階市場發展，使終
端產品售價提升。  
南亞科預期供需緊缺的狀況預計將持續延續至 2027 年甚至 2028 年初。南
亞科目前的策略則是技術自主、長約鎖定與利潤導向的防禦性資本支出，而
非衝產量、殺價拼市占。南亞科目前簽署長期供應協議 (LTA) 的產能約占總
出貨量的 50~70% 。不過南亞科位元出貨量已達高點，現有廠區在 4Q27 新
廠量產前產能維持滿載，出貨量維持平穩走勢，加上公司庫存處於低檔，預
估南亞科 2027 年 bit shipment 持平，價格走勢維持高檔，使南亞科毛利率
水準有望優於 2026 年，預估南亞科 2027 年稅後 EPS 127.64 元。

10 群益投顧 
  【投資評等說明】  
  評等  定義  
  強力買進 (Strong Buy)  首次評等潛在上漲空間 ≥ 35%  
  買進 (Buy)  15% ≤首次評等潛在上漲空間 <35%  
  區間操作 (Trading Buy) 5% ≤首次評等潛在上漲空間 <15%

中立 (Neutral)  
無法由基本面給予投資評等  
預期近期股價將處於盤整  
建議降低持股

【投資評等調整機制】  
  投資評等係「首次」給予特定個股投資評等時，其前一日收盤價相較 3 個月
目標價之潛在上漲空間計算而得。個股投資評等分為四個等級，定義如上。
爾後的投資評等係依循「首次評等」，直到停止推薦。   
「強力買進」、「買進」及「區間操作」均有 upside 目標價。差別僅在於，
首次評等時潛在報酬率不同。「中立」則無目標價。  
一旦我們給予特定個股「強力買進」 、 「買進」或「區間操作」之投資評等，
就是責任的開始，爾後將透過各式研究報告作定期性、持續性追蹤基本面及
股價變化，直到停止推薦。  
停止推薦情境：  
1.  達目標價。  
2.  雖未達原訂目標價，但檢視基本面、訊息面、籌碼面等多方訊息，研
判股價上漲空間已然有限，將適時出具降評報告。  
3.  推薦後股價不漲反跌。

【免責聲明】  
  本研究報告僅提供予特定人之客戶作為參考資料「非經同意不得轉載」 。我
們並不確保此資訊的完整性與正確性，投資人應了解，報告中有關未來預測
之陳述可能不會實現，因而不應被依賴。而且此報告並非根據特定投資目的
或依預定對象之財務狀況所撰寫出來的，因此，此研究報告的目的，既非對
投資人於買賣證券、選擇權、期貨或其他證券相關之衍生性商品提供詢價服
務，亦非作為進行交易的要約。投資人應注意到相關證券之價值及收益，可
能會有無預警地上升或下降，產生投資回報金額可能比原始投資來得少的
情形。

### Page 2

Taiwan Electronics & Semiconductors
12 October 2026 Citi Research
2
PCB and substrate- 3Q26 was a strong quarter for substrate and CCL sectors, with
demand continuously piling up. Unimicron posted +23% QoQ sales growth in 3Q26,
beating Citi/consensus forecasts by 4%/6%. We expect 4Q26E ABF sales
momentum to be even stronger vs 3Q considering all the AI chips ramping up
together. CCL names also saw strong momentum across the board thanks to
enhanced product mix and benefit from price hike in 2Q26. We expect CCL sector to

see another round of price hikes from Nov onwards. For PCB, GCE’s Sept sales were
impacted by flooding in Thailand, which led to logistics disruption, while Tripod
posted record monthly sales backed by memory demand and early pull-in before
China Golden Week. With TPCA Show coming next week, we think
substrate/CCL/PCB sectors might see some event catalysts in the near term.
However, from a fundamental perspective, we still prefer substrate
(Unimicron>NYPCB>Kinsus) > CCL (EMC> TUC) > PCB.

PC/NB Supply chain - For NB ODMs, overall NB shipments see MoM increase in Sep
(+32% MoM) due to the quarter end inventory build; combined 3Q NB shipments
were down 17% QoQ due to  the early pull-in in 1H. We forecast 4Q26E NB shipments
to further decline -7% QoQ/-28% YoY given the muted demand on rising retails
prices and the increasing constraint in components procurement, While the price
hike effect in the NB products should partially offset shipment decline, we expect

increasing margins dilution impact to the NB ODM supply chains.
For lens components - Largan reported relatively muted sales results in Sep at
NT$5,779m (+15% MoM/-7% YoY); combined 3Q revenues are below market
estimates while Genius’ 3Q revenues were slightly better than expectation. Despite
the near-term sales performance, we see more upside opportunity coming from
their new development in optical business in the longer term. We expect Genius’
project with a leading US company on optical connector and ferrule to start

shipment in 1H27. For Largan, we expect it to benefit most from FA/FAU for CPO
market. We see its current engagement with the leading US client progressing
smoothly, as evidenced by its continued new land expansion for future capacity
build-out (see our recent report).
Thermal – Sep-26 sales as reported by thermal players (AVC & Auras) were up from
Aug at +6% MoM/+42% YoY. While AVC delivered another record month thanks to
solid ASIC demand and initial Vera Rubin shipments, Auras experienced MoM

decline due to client deferring pull-ins and Thailand flooding. Going forward, we
preliminarily expect both companies to see sequential revenue growth in 4Q26E,
driven by the AWS Trainium transition from air to liquid cooling, ramp-up of the TPU
and Vera Rubin projects.
Power – Delta reported MoM revenue recovery in Sep-26 (+1% MoM/+14% YoY), with
power electronics down 4% MoM. 3Q26 revenues were up 8% QoQ but below our
and Street’s expectation. We expect Delta to regain momentum with double-digit

QoQ growth in 4Q26E as ±400V enters the initial transition period, while in the long
term, HVDC is still progressing gradually while Delta maintains its technology
leadership. For BBU, we see Sep sales showing mixed momentum yet resilient BBU
demand remains intact. Dynapack’s Sep-26 sales declined 34% y/y and 54% m/m
to NT$776mn, reflecting 1) weaker PC shipments amid quarter-end inventory
adjustments and 2) deferred shipments of one BBU product, broadly consistent with

the near-term weakness flagged in our Aug-26 sales note. We expect a meaningful
sales rebound from Oct helped by shipments of new higher-power BBU products.
Meanwhile, AES maintained solid momentum with Sep-26 sales rising 25% y/y (+1%
m/m), suggesting resilient underlying BBU demand. Similarly, STL (4931.TWO; not
covered) delivered robust 176% y/y growth, further reinforcing our positive view on
the sector. We retain our constructive stance on BBU names, supported by

sustained demand and upcoming product ramps
Passive components - Yageo reported trended up monthly sales in Sep-26, as
demand in Europe and US recovered after the summer holiday, on top of sustained
AI demand. 3Q26 revenues were slightly above cons forecasts. Current quarter top
line still beats mgmt. 's "moderate growth" guidance. We believe that growth was
again broad-based across commodity and specialty products, and driven by both
volume and price. We expect a more visible ASP uplift in 4Q26E as specialty and

contract-based business could slow vs the spot and distributor channels.

participate when the analyst has a positive view of the company.

Guide to Citi Research Fundamental Research Investment Ratings:
Citi Research stock recommendations include an investment rating and an optional risk rating to highlight high risk stocks.

Risk rating takes into account both price volatility and fundamental criteria. Stocks will either have no risk rating or a High risk
rating assigned.
Investment Ratings: Citi Research investment ratings are Buy, Neutral and Sell. Our ratings are a function of analyst
expectations of expected total return ("ETR") and risk. ETR is the sum of the forecast price appreciation (or depreciation) plus
the dividend yield for a stock within the next 12 months.  The target price is based on a 12 month time horizon. The Investment

rating definitions are: Buy (1) ETR of 15% or more or 25% or more for High risk stocks; and Sell (3) for negative ETR. Any covered
stock not assigned a Buy or a Sell is a Neutral (2). For stocks rated Neutral (2), if an analyst believes that there are insufficient
valuation drivers and/or investment catalysts to derive a positive or negative investment view, they may elect with the approval
of Citi Research management not to assign a target price and, thus, not derive an ETR. Citi Research may suspend its rating

and target price and assign “Rating Suspended” status for regulatory and/or internal policy reasons. Citi Research may also
suspend its rating and target price and assign "Under Review" status for other exceptional circumstances (e.g. lack of
information critical to the analyst's thesis, trading suspension) affecting the company and/or trading in the company's
securities. In both such situations, the rating and target price will show as “—“ and “-“ respectively in the rating history price

chart. Prior to 11 April 2022 Citi Research assigned “Under Review” status to both situations and prior to 11 Nov 2020 only in
exceptional circumstances. As soon as practically possible, the analyst will publish a note re-establishing a rating and
investment thesis. Investment ratings are determined by the ranges described above at the time of initiation of coverage, a
change in investment and/or risk rating, or a change in target price (subject to limited management discretion). At times, the

expected total returns may fall outside of these ranges because of market price movements and/or other short-term volatility
or trading patterns. Such interim deviations will be permitted but will become subject to review by Research Management.
Your decision to buy or sell a security should be based upon your personal investment objectives and should be made only
after evaluating the stock's expected performance and risk.
Catalyst Watch/Short Term Views (“STV”) Ratings Disclosure:

Catalyst Watch and STV Upside/Downside calls: Citi Research may also include a Catalyst Watch or STV Upside or Downside
call to indicate the analyst expects the share price to rise (fall) in absolute terms over a specified period of 30 or 90 days in
reaction to one or more specific near-term catalysts or events impacting the company or the market. A Catalyst Watch will be
published when Analyst confidence is high that an impact to share price will occur; it will be a STV when confidence level is

moderate. A Catalyst Watch or STV Upside/Downside call will automatically expire at the end of the specified 30/90 day
period. The Catalyst Watch will also be automatically removed if share price performance (calculated at market close) exceeds
15% against the direction of the call (unless over-ridden by the analyst). The analyst may also remove a Catalyst Watch or STV
call prior to the end of the specified period in a published research note. A Catalyst Watch/STV Upside or Downside call may be

### Page 2

Key Takeaways from Company Meetings

Key Takeaways from Applied Materials Meeting 
Participants: Kevin Moraes, VP , Strategy and Marketing; Sree Kesapragada, VP , 
Advanced Packaging; Mike Sullivan, Head of Investor Relations 
Investor questions mainly focused on clarifications tied to the company’s investor event 
and financial targets. On revenue and capital intensity, management noted that 
semiconductor industry pricing is a key variable that it cannot control - but has high 
confidence in its framework tied to increasing WFE intensity per node - and these 
increases have already been programmatically locked in by customers today. On 
Applied’s target of 200bps of market share gain, management noted the complex set of 
mix effects involved in the calculation, but noted the substantial impact from China 
export control exclusions. Relative to the company’s newly articulated gross margin 
target of 54% by 2030, Applied believes there is the potential for upside to this target - 
but pointed out that the vast majority of its costs are variable in nature. On its optical 
tool portfolio, Applied believes that it can leverage its expertise and market position in 
e-beam technology to expand into this adjacent $9bn market. On AGS, management has 
high confidence in its outlook for mid-teens long term revenue growth - but noted that 
its services attach rate is higher on its newer tools relative to its installed fleet. 
Key Takeaways from Lam Research Meeting 
Participants: Ram Ganesh - VP of Investor Relations 
Consistent with prior commentary, Lam expects 8-10 new fab projects to come online in 
2027 - and currently expects product growth to be driven by DRAM, followed by 
advanced foundry/logic, followed by NAND flash. The company has not specified a 
timeframe for achieving its new long-term gross margin target of ~55%. However, we 
expect this to be achieved over several years, and see it tied more to product mix 
(particularly with new products such as Akara) and overseas operational manufacturing 
efficiencies rather than like-for-like pricing. Management remains constructive on the 
company’s competitive positioning in Logic, DRAM, and advanced packaging - 
particularly as etch and deposition intensity continues to grow with a variety of new 
device structures coming into the mix - including hard masks, surface prep, TSVs, and 
other materials engineering steps. 
Key Takeaways from Teradyne Meeting 
Participants: Roy Chorev - VP & GM of Compute Test Division; Amy McAndrews - VP of 
Investor Relations 
Teradyne announced a strategic partnership with and investment in FormFactor at 
SEMICON West. Teradyne remains committed to an open ecosystem of probe card 
vendors, but believes Form’s strong position in silicon photonics warrants a closer 
relationship that enables Teradyne to commit additional engineering platform resources. 
Regarding its XPU business, Teradyne continues to expect to gain merchant GPU share in 
the long run - and noted that Rubin test times are nearly 2X Blackwell given the near 
doubling in transistor count. Teradyne is also pursuing the tester opportunity at Intel, 
which is focused on increasing its exposure to external vendors (currently using an 
14 October 2026   2
Goldman Sachs Americas Technology: SemiconductorsFor the exclusive use of KEVINLU@LENOVO.COM

### Page 3

internal solution) beginning with its foundry business, where Teradyne sees a
medium-term opportunity. In its memory business, Teradyne expects minimal impact
from customer de-specing of HBM as its demand is tied primarily to unit volume rather
than stack height. In co-packaged optics, Teradyne continues to see a very strong
product ramp opportunity, although given the early stages of development, there are
likely to be significant shifts between testing intensity at different insertions during the

manufacturing process.
Key Takeaways from Camtek Investor Meeting
Participants: Lior Aviram - Executive Chairman; Rafi Amit - CEO; Ramy Langer -
President & COO; Moshe Eisenberg - CFO; Eyal Segev - Marketing Director
We attended Camtek’s Investor Meeting at SEMICON. Our key takeaways are as follows:
Long-term financial targets: In conjunction with its investor meeting, Camtek n
pre-announced 3Q26 revenue of $160 mn –$161 mn (above its prior guidance),

guided to ~$180 mn for 4Q26, and provided an initial 2027 revenue outlook of $800
mn based on orders received to date. Management laid out a long-term revenue
target of $1.2 billion in about three years, with a gross margin target of 55%-56%
and operating margin target of 34%-36%.
Advanced packaging remains the key growth driver: Camtek stressed that n
advanced packaging remains its core business, accounting for ~80% of recent
orders, and expects advanced packaging wafer growth to run at about twice the

broader industry rate through 2031 (13% vs. 7% CAGR). Within advanced packaging,
the company expects the high-end of the market (which accounts for the vast
majority of its business) to grow at a ~24% CAGR through 2031, driven by AI, silicon
photonics, denser interconnects, and increasingly complex inspection and metrology
requirements.
Technology and product roadmap centered on inspection and specialized n
metrology: Camtek’s roadmap remains centered on its margin-accretive Hawk and

Eagle products for inspection (~85% of its 2026 mix), as well as its metrology
platforms. Separately, management also noted it is embedding AI into detection and
classification and plans to launch AI-enabled software in 2027 (which it attributed to
its Visual Layer acquisition).
M&A strategy focused on capability acquisition and strategic expansion: Camtek n
noted that it is pursuing both smaller acquisitions that add technology and/or talent
to its core business, as well as larger strategic transactions that broaden its offering,

while emphasizing that its $1.2 bn revenue target and financial model do not assume
meaningful M&A activity.
Key Takeaways from Onto Innovation Meeting
Participants: Brian Roberts - CFO; Sidney Ho - VP of Investor Relations
Onto remains very constructive on the demand outlook through 2H26 and heading into
2027 across a range of demand drivers - with growth likely to accelerate next year given
the growth of incremental market opportunities. In HBM, Onto believes its business is

likely to be minimally impacted by any “de-spec” activity by XPU vendors, as its tool
14 October 2026   3
Goldman Sachs Americas Technology: SemiconductorsFor the exclusive use of KEVINLU@LENOVO.COM
e92c7a75ab8b4efbba794e6b187208c8

### Page 4

demand is far more driven by unit volume than stack height. The company continues to
see CoWoS as a key demand driver that is increasingly driven by OSAT partners rather
than TSMC; Onto sees EMIB-T as a significant opportunity that promises to be material in
2028. From a competitive standpoint, Onto remains highly confident in its market share
position, including at TSMC - and sees no imminent impact from potential product
launches by competitors. On margins, management remains confident in its target of

350bps of gross margin expansion, which it believes can be achieved solely by product
mix and efficiencies in its manufacturing operations. The company believes it has room
to grow revenue substantially from projected levels, and does not see itself as capacity
constrained.
Key Takeaways from MKS Instruments Meeting
Participants: John Lee - President & CEO; Paretosh Misra - VP of Investor Relations
MKS believes the industry is in the early-to-middle stages of a substantial cyclical

industry upturn driven by AI and datacenter demand. The company is currently
preparing for a potential WFE spending envelope of up to $300bn in 2028, and
potentially higher over time. In the near-term, management expects the company to
potentially outgrow its customers (2Q26 was the first time the company delivered Y/Y
Semiconductor revenue growth in excess of them) given supply chain inventory effects.
MKS believes it has enough capacity to support WFE of ~$250bn with its Penang facility

(with a surge capacity of another 20%), but may need to expand capacity further if
these forecasts come to fruition. Importantly, the company does not believe it is
constraining its customers’ growth plans today. MKS has not taken significant pricing
actions, but could do so selectively in order to capture the value it delivers. Management
believes its product portfolio is well positioned, but would consider a possible
divestiture of its specialty industrial product lines at an attractive valuation.

### Page 5

Valuation/Risks

AMAT (Buy, on CL): Our 12-month price target of $670 is based on a 32X P/E multiple 
applied to our normalized EPS estimate of $21.00. Key downside risks include: (1) 
incremental export restrictions; (2) local China-based supplier share gains. 
LRCX (Buy): Our price target is $380, which is based on a 35X multiple applied to our 
normalized EPS forecast of $10.75. Key downside risks: (1) softer DRAM/HBM demand; 
(2) delays in planned clean room and fab expansions; (3) lower-than-expected content 
gains from advanced packaging and 3D architectures. 
TER (Buy): Our 12-month price target is $465, which is based on a 37X P/E multiple 
applied to our normalized EPS forecast of $12.50. Key downside risks: (1) delays in the 
revenue ramp with a merchant GPU customer, (2) significant quarterly volatility due to 
the lumpy nature of large AI-related infrastructure projects, (3) slower-than-expected 
recovery in Mobile. 
ONTO (Buy): Our12-month price target is $400, which is based on 32X our normalized 
EPS estimate of $12.50. Our target P/E is in line with peer multiples, and it is on the 
higher end of ONTO’s historical median, given recent momentum around HPC. Key 
upside/downside risks: (1) additional volume purchase agreements and accelerated 
customer adoption of Dragonfly G5; (2) shift towards OSATs or domestic Chinese 
manufacturers; (3) competitor launching a rival tool. 
CAMT (Neutral): Our 12-m price target of $160 is based on a 38X P/E multiple applied 
to our normalized EPS of $4.20. Key upside/downside risks: (1) notable push-outs in 
chiplet architectures; (2) failure to pass on higher pricing; (3) better-than-expected 
growth in HPC-driven revenue in the medium-term. 
MKSI (Sell): Our 12-month price target is $270, which is based on a 20X P/E multiple 
applied to our normalized EPS of $13.50. Key upside risks: (1) rebound in the NAND 
market from an equipment ordering perspective; (2) greater realization of synergies 
from the Atotech acquisition; (3) any material pickup in momentum for the company’s 
Semiconductor business due to broader WFE trends.

6426 統新 
(Apogee Optocom Co.)

報告日期： 2026/10/19 研 究 員：方姿婷  (Lesley Fang) 
壹、投 資 建 議 
重點摘要： 
 統新 2Q26 營收為 2.08 億元，+34.7%QoQ、+82.4%YoY，毛利率
躍升至 56.20%，稅後淨利 0.86 億元，單季稅後 EPS+2.22 元。
2Q26 受惠生產效率提升、產能滿載毛利率躍升。 
 AI 伺服器帶動統新的 DWDM 產品延伸至 AI 資料中心 DCI 主要
骨幹濾片，光纖波段分離與光纖傳輸頻寬增加，會需要統新薄
膜濾光片作為訊號傳輸強化。除 Scale-Across 長距離骨幹，分
散式 AI 數據中心，專門做長距離光通訊傳輸 DWDM 市場，
2026~2031 年每年維持 31%年複合成長。 
 1.6T 光纖收發模組 ， 未來 2 年呈倍數增長 。 1.6T 光纖收發模組，
在後端 PD(接收端)改採 200G PD，會用到 8 片統新之薄膜濾光
片。

一. 統新 2Q26 營收與財報優於市場預期，3Q26 續強 
統新 2Q26 營收為 2.08 億元，+34.7%QoQ、+82.4%YoY，毛利率躍
升至 56.20%，稅後淨利 0.86 億元，單季稅後 EPS+2.22 元。主要受
惠於 AI 資料中心互連需求，使得統新產線維持滿載。展望 3Q26
預估統新營收為 2.42 億元，+16.35%QoQ，毛利率 56.58%，稅後淨
利 1.06 億元，單季稅後 EPS+2.76 元。統新預計產能增加 30%，以
因應客戶需求。

二. 2026 年 DWDM 隨 AI 骨幹網絡建置，濾片量增價漲 
統新高密度波長多工器( D W D M，D e n s e  W a v e l e n g t h  D i v i s i o n  
Multiplexing)，至少可傳輸 16 種不同顏色的光纖，最高可傳輸 128
種不同顏色之光纖，同一條主幹網路上，可以傳輸的頻寬因此加
大，相當具有傳輸效率。DWDM 於發送端將不同波長的光信號組
合起來(覆用, MUX)，並耦合到光纜線路上的同一根光纖中，進行
傳輸，在接收端又將多個波長的光信號分開(解覆用, DEMUX)，並
作進一步處理，恢復出原波長信號後送入不同的終端接收器。統
新的 DWDM 產品延伸至 AI 資料中心 DCI 主要骨幹濾片，波段分
離 ( S -B a n d、C - B a n d 傳 輸 核 心、L -B a nd 容 量 擴 充、S -B a nd 未 來 預 留、
U-Band 8nm 用於 OTDR 光纖監控偵測主幹網路是否具有斷點或需
要救援)，統新以單通道 100GHz 去細分 DWDM 濾光片，統新推出
新品，50GHz 間隔濾光片，支援的通道數量提升至 96Channels。 
投資評等：買進 
目標價位：375.00 元

三.  AI 伺服器高速傳輸，帶動光通訊主被動元件量增 
Scale-Across 長距離骨幹，分散式 AI 數據中心，專門做長距離光
通訊傳輸 DWDM 市場，2026~2031 年，DWDM 市場每年維持 31%
年 複合 成長。此 外，受惠 AI 伺服 器傳 輸速 率提升，如：Broadcom、
Marvell 等國際晶片廠商，於 2026 年起已推出 800G /1.6T Switch 晶
片組，等同於 AI 伺服器後端 Ports 支援的光纖通訊傳輸會一併升
級至 800G 與 1.6T 傳輸速率。Yole 報告指出，1.6T 光纖收發模組
出貨量，2026 年/2027 年/2028 年預估量 15~20M/55~60M/85~100M，
1.6T 光纖收發模組 ， 未來 2 年內呈倍數增長 。 1.6T 光纖收發模組，
在後端 PD(接收端)改採 200G PD ， 會用到 8 片統新之薄膜濾光片。
在薄膜濾光片鍍膜層數 ， Scale-Across 與 NPO 所需薄膜濾光片層數
約 150L~200L，未來發展至 CPO 時鍍膜層數會再增加 8L，統新為
此預計多準備 10 條產線預計開發 250L+規格，予台系知名晶圓代
工廠商。統新指出，Scale-Out 濾片鍍膜代工量增加，是替中系系
統廠代工(層數約 100~150L)。Scale-Across 濾片鍍膜層數會大於
200L，甚至高達 250L，目前訂單能見度高，涵蓋 1.)同業產能難以
支應，Cohr 交給統新、2.)中系系統廠商加單、3.)台系網通廠商於
低軌衛星鍍膜(LEO 非光學鍍膜) 。統新毛利率之所以跳升，是先前
客戶給統新的試產量僅 20~30 片，2Q26 起量產量增至 K 級別，出
貨量增加、規模經濟效應之下，單月營收損平點(評估為 0.4~0.5
億元)，2Q26 起月營收顯著跳升至 0.6~0.7 億元。預期此營運動能
至少能見度到 4Q26，但由於 DWDM 交期延長至 6~8 個月，預估
2027 年產業供需偏向緊俏。

四.投資建議：買進，目標價 375 元 
統新競爭優勢在於其生產高品質高效率之薄膜濾光片篩選特定輻
射波段的光學元件，能篩選光的強度、改變光譜成分或限定震動
截面，藉由 CWDM/DWDM 技術使相同數據集中於同一根光纖中 
傳輸，增大傳輸容量進而提高光纖的使用效率，生產良率逐步提
升且具佔有一定之市場地位。預估統新 2026 年營收 8.56 億元，
+75.77%YoY，毛利率 53.43%，稅後淨利 3.32 億元，稅後 EPS 為
+8.63 元。展望 2027 年，隨產能積極擴充之下，預估統新年營收
為 17.02 億元，+98.83%YoY ，毛利率預估為 54.85% ，稅後淨利 5.57
億元，稅後 EPS+14.47 元。考量基礎建設帶動 DWDM 規格升級，
AI 伺服器傳輸速率提升因應 1.6T 傳輸薄膜濾光片用量同步增長，
給予統新投資建議買進，目標價 375 元(26.00 X2027 EPS)。

一.公司簡介

統新(6426)為生產精密光學元件，以及高密度分波多工器(DWDM)
關鍵元件─薄膜濾光片(TFF)之專業廠商。各產品線營收占比為主
動光學元件佔比低於 15%、被動光學元件：80~85%、光學鍍膜元
件與代工低於 10%。主要產品 DWDM 濾光片 (Dense Wavelength 
Division Multiplexer，即為高密度波長多工分工器)技術可以大幅提
高光纖通訊系統之頻寬，成為光纖通訊系統的市場中關注的焦
點。統新國內主要競爭對手為東典光電科技(股)，國外廠商包含
II-IV Cohernet(美國)、Iridian Technologies(加拿大)等廠商投入相關
產品研發及生產。

圖一、光通訊元件

資料來源：公司、宏遠投顧整理

二.26 年營運概況及 26 年展望 
1.統新 DWDM 技術層層堆疊良率優異  
統新主要產品為薄膜濾光片即為篩選特定輻射波段的光學元件，
能篩選光的強度、改變光譜成分或限定震動截面，藉由不同光線
原理而選擇通過的光源(紅、藍、綠光) ，且藉由 CWDM/DWDM 技
術使相同數據集中於同一根光纖中傳輸，增大傳輸容量進而提高
光纖的使用效率。

2.因應 AI 資料中心基建商機，薄膜濾光片產品走向更高規  
回顧 5G 時期的薄膜濾光片，支援頻段增加，切割更細，中心波

可望取得一定市場地位。

### Page 5

5
Gogolook三大事業體
資料來源：Gogolook、群益投顧彙整
⚫ Gogolook 是全球領先的信任科技 (TrustTech) 公司，運用自有防詐資料庫與 AI 技
術，提供消費者防詐、企業端防詐與金融科技服務。Gogolook 擁有全球最齊全的
通訊與數位防詐數據資料庫，涵蓋電話號碼、網址、和虛擬貨幣錢包地址等。

### Page 6

6
whoscall
⚫ whoscall 為 Gogolook 所
開發出的個人數位防詐
APP，全球下載量已突破
1億次，月活躍用戶超過
1,800萬，主要服務市場
涵蓋台灣、泰國、日本、
韓國、香港、巴西、馬
來西亞、菲律賓等。
資料來源：Gogolook、群益投顧彙整

### Page 7

7
whoscall海外市場持續擴張
⚫ whoscall海外市場擴張成效卓越，海外營收占比於2025年已突破63%。泰國為
Gogolook最大的海外市場，其用戶規模、營收貢獻已超越台灣，但仍具備很大的
成長空間；公司於曼谷擁有龐大用戶基礎，目前正進軍泰國二、三線城市。菲律
賓與馬來西亞等地已具備百萬等級的活躍用戶，後續成長可期。
⚫ Gogolook於09/2026已正式和泰國政府退休基金(Government Pension Fund，GPF)簽
署MOU，並與國家跨行交易管理與交換公司(National ITMX)及泰國存款保障機構
(Deposit Protection Agency，DPA)合作，將whoscall防詐服務導入GPF體系，涵蓋
退休人員及現職公務員(超過120萬名會員)。此合作有助於同步推廣泰國當地B2C、
B2B業務。
⚫ 為了有效控制獲客成本，Gogolook在海外減少依賴成效較低的數位廣告，轉而與
當地政府、銀行及電信商合作。此外，考量到東南亞信用卡普及率較低，

Gogolook在泰國率先推出的「電信帳單扣款」訂閱模式大獲成功，目前此模式也
與台灣遠傳電信合作，藉此攻佔中高年齡層客群。

### Page 8

8
whoscall海外市場持續擴張
⚫ 針對品牌偏好較強或市場規模較小的國家(如日本與新加坡)，則透過白牌授權策略，
協助當地業者開發自有防詐App並從中分潤。
⚫ whoscall於02/2026~05/2026與日本Rakuten Mobile進行合作，推出「騷擾電話・簡
訊防護」活動，提供申請用戶免費試用，目前開始有試用用戶轉為付費訂閱，有
望挹注2H26日本市場消費者防詐業務成長動能。

### Page 10

10
Gogolook收購ScamAdviser
⚫ 企業端防詐方面，由於各國法規有加重企業防詐責任的趨勢，Gogolook將此業務
視為長期成長動能潛力來源。Gogolook於2024年收購具備超過6,000萬筆網域資料
庫的ScamAdviser後，成功拓展歐美市場。儘管2025年因主動調整部分低毛利率業
務，導致ScamAdviser營收下滑，但其實質獲利反而增加。
資料來源：Gogolook、群益投顧彙整
⚫ ScamAdviser成立於荷蘭阿姆斯
特丹，為全球防詐聯盟GASA
基石成員。ScamAdviser以AI演
算法與資料庫為基礎，開發自
有的網站風險評級技術，與
Gogolook的企業防詐解決方案
與電話號碼資料庫整合，為全
球最完整的通訊與數位防詐數
據資料庫。

### Page 11

11
Gogolook企業端防詐服務
⚫ 企業端服務主要分為「資料庫授權」與「商譽保護」兩大主軸：(1) 防詐情資方案，
為一種資料庫授權模式，將whoscall的「電話/簡訊」資料庫以及ScamAdviser的
「網域」資料庫，透過API、SDK方式授權給企業。(2) 商譽保護服務，企業的外
部防詐監控中心，協助企業或政府主動在網路上巡邏，偵測並下架冒用其品牌的
假投資廣告、假粉專或釣魚網站。
資料來源：Gogolook、群益投顧彙整

### Page 12

12
Agent looker
⚫ 因應Agentic AI時代來臨，Gogolook於09/2026推出新產品Agent looker，工作原理
是在AI agent實際造訪或讀取網頁前，即時進行風險檢測並攔截惡意請求，可有效
避免AI agent被詐騙的風險。
⚫ 核心防護機制與功能：(1)毫秒級即時掃描，當AI Agent讀取外部網頁或執行URL
請求時，Agent Looker會在毫秒內對網頁封包與內容進行深度檢查，攔截惡意腳本、
釣魚網站以及針對 AI 模型的提示詞注入(Prompt Injection)攻擊。(2)Gogolook威脅
情資資料庫驅動。系統底層串接 Gogolook 累積多年的全球防詐騙、釣魚網站與風
險網域黑名單。基於海量真實風險樣本的數據庫，使其能快速且精準地辨識異常
行為。(3)活動稽核日誌。提供視覺化的管理儀表板，完整記錄AI Agent的所有外
部網路互動軌跡，包含造訪過的網址、讀取的網頁片段及最終的安全判定結果，
確保AI運作透明且可追溯。
⚫ Agent looker目前處於封閉測試(Private Beta)階段。

資料來源：Gogolook、群益投顧彙整

### Page 13

13
金融科技-袋鼠金融
⚫ 金融科技為Gogolook於疫情期間因應線上詐騙激增而開創的新業務，包含全台灣
流量最大的金融媒合平台「袋鼠金融Roo.Cash」，以及專攻小額借款的「JUJI招財
麻吉」。袋鼠金融 Roo.Cash與國內金融機構合作，提供200件以上的金融商品方案
智慧比較與媒合。智能問答服務「Roo AI」，可協助用戶在信用卡、貸款、保險、
投資理財與民生優惠資訊中，快速找到最適合的選項。若順利成交，Gogolook即
可藉此獲得媒合收入。
資料來源：Gogolook、群益投顧彙整

### Page 14

14
金融科技-JUJI招財麻吉
⚫ JUJI招財麻吉鎖定10萬元以下、傳統銀行不願承作的利基市場，自行舉辦機車貸
款、手機貸款等業務。該業務最核心的優勢在於將whoscall的通話行為數據轉化為
AI風控模型，不僅可嚴格控制核貸率，更將90天延滯率壓在極低水準。目前JUJI
招財麻吉應收分期款餘額持續增加，公司同時也把純風控模型顧問服務輸出至海
外市場，進一步擴大變現能力，2Q26已成功在泰國市場簽訂首批企業訂單。
資料來源：Gogolook、群益投顧彙整

Gogolook營收續增，毛利率維持高檔

Gogolook營業費用控管得宜

Document 231: AI算力需求带动PCB设备增长，大族激光Q3营收超80亿元，净利同比大增137%｜财报见闻

### Page 1

⼤族激光第三季度营收 80.61 亿元，同⽐增⻓ 58.07% ；归⺟净利润 8.89 亿元，同⽐增⻓
137.03% 。前三季度营收增⻓ 68.92% ，扣⾮净利润同⽐增⻓ 304.87% ，盈利增⻓主要来⾃
主营业务。同期，经营活动现⾦流净额转正⾄ 2.27 亿元，但应收账款增⾄ 132.01 亿元、存
货升⾄ 77.48 亿元。
摘要：
⼤族激光 2026 年前三季度营收和净利润均实现较快增⻓。财报显⽰，公司前三季度实现营业收
⼊ 214.74 亿元，同⽐增⻓ 68.92% ；归属于上市公司股东的净利润为 21.77 亿元，同⽐增⻓
152.19% 。第三季度单季营收 80.61 亿元、净利润 8.89 亿元，同⽐分别增⻓ 58.07% 和 137.03% ，
延续了上半年的增⻓势头。
盈利指标⽅⾯，前三季度扣除⾮经常性损益后的净利润为 23.01 亿元，同⽐增⻓ 304.87% ，⾼于
归⺟净利润增速。同期，公司基本每股收益为 2.11 元，上年同期为 0.84 元；加权平均净资产收益

率为 10.39% ，同⽐提⾼ 5.37 个百分点。
报告期内，⼤族激光⼦公司⼤族数控在⾹港联交所主板上市，募集资⾦近 50 亿元。受此影响，
公司少数股东权益由年初的 16.43 亿元增⾄ 40.87 亿元，总资产则由 382 亿元增⾄ 508.50 亿元。

### Page 2

营收与利润：收⼊增⻓带动盈利提升
前三季度，⼤族激光实现营业收⼊ 214.74 亿元，较上年同期的 127.13 亿元增加 87.61 亿元，同⽐
增⻓ 68.92% 。同期，公司营业利润为 29.77 亿元，同⽐增⻓ 189.57% ；利润总额为 29.83 亿元，
同⽐增⻓约 190% 。
从利润构成看，前三季度扣⾮净利润同⽐增⻓ 304.87% ，⾼于归⺟净利润 152.19% 的增幅，表明
扣除⾮经常性损益后，公司盈利增⻓仍较为明显。报告期内，公司⾮经常性损益合计约为 -1.24
亿元，其中持有⾦融资产公允价值变动带来约 2.02 亿元损失，对当期利润形成⼀定拖累。相⽐之
下，上年同期相关公允价值变动为正贡献，影响了两期净利润增速的可⽐性。
成本⽅⾯，前三季度营业成本为 138.47 亿元，同⽐增⻓ 61.53% ，低于营业收⼊增速。销售费
⽤、管理费⽤和研发费⽤分别为 12.84 亿元、 12.21 亿元和 18.36 亿元，合计约 43.41 亿元。相关
费⽤增速低于收⼊增速，在⼀定程度上反映出收⼊规模扩⼤后费⽤占⽐有所下降，但后续仍需

结合⽑利率、费⽤率及业务结构变化判断盈利改善的持续性。
现⾦流：经营活动现⾦流转正，投资⽀出增加
前三季度，公司经营活动产⽣的现⾦流量净额为 2.27 亿元，上年同期为净流出 8.62 亿元，同⽐改
善约 10.89 亿元。同期，销售商品、提供劳务收到的现⾦为 161.75 亿元，同⽐增⻓约 57% ，经营
活动现⾦流较上年同期有所改善。
不过，经营活动现⾦流净额仍明显低于同期归⺟净利润，反映出利润与现⾦回收之间仍存在差
距。随着收⼊规模扩⼤，应收账款同步增⻓，后续回款情况仍值得关注。
投资活动⽅⾯，公司前三季度现⾦流量净额为 -13.98 亿元，上年同期为净流⼊ 7.58 亿元。现⾦流
变化与股权投资及固定资产投⼊增加有关，相关投资涉及安腾创新、永通智造、胜宏科技、景
旺电⼦等企业，以及华东区域总部基地建设。
筹资活动现⾦流量净额为 37.85 亿元，主要受⼤族数控赴港上市募资等因素影响。期末货币资⾦
余额为 98.82 亿元，较年初增加 18.69 亿元。需要注意的是，⼦公司上市募资属于资本性资⾦来

源，与主营业务经营产⽣的现⾦流性质不同。
资产负债表：规模扩张，应收账款与存货同步增⻓
截⾄报告期末，⼤族激光总资产增⾄ 508.50 亿元，较年初增⻓ 32.95% 。除经营规模扩⼤外，⼤
族数控上市带来的资⾦注⼊及少数股东权益增加，也是资产规模扩张的重要因素。

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
ECONOMIC INSIGHT
• Recent revisions to US PCE inflation, UK productivity
and Canadian population estimates are reminders that
statistical measurement is imperfect. Revisions are both a
fact of life and, when they improve measurement, desirable.
• Australian data will also be revised over time, though

not necessarily in the same ways as these latest revisions
abroad. The ABS tends to favour quality over speed,
which reduces the need for early revisions as more data
become available. But revisions can still matter. Population
estimates implied in the initial releases of labour market and
GDP data are especially likely to be revised in the near term.
• Estimates of current trends can change as future data are
published. Shifting seasonal patterns are another source

of revision, especially for monthly CPI components with
short histories. Series that are derived from multiple other
imperfectly measured series are more prone to revision
than quantities that can be measured directly. All these
considerations point to a need for humility when using
recent data to make strong policy pronouncements.
On September 30, the Fed’s preferred measure of inflation,
PCE, was revised down. New methods and data sources were
applied to three components of services inflation (financial

advice, software and legal services), large enough to result in
downward revisions to the overall read on core PCE inflation.
These changes had been pre-announced in June and were
therefore not a surprise. The CPI measure of inflation was not
affected, nor should the revisions materially change your view
of US inflationary pressures.
Some revisions are more consequential for interpretation and
policy. A couple of weeks before the US PCE revision, it was

revealed that the UK Office for National Statistics was revising
down its estimates of average hours worked per worker. The
result was that measured productivity growth was nowhere
near as weak as had been widely believed. As my friend and
counterpart, TS Lombard’s Chief Economist Freya Beamish
commented on social media, “Oh well. Not like any major policy
decisions were taken on the basis of those numbers.” Quite.
Also in September, Statistics Canada revised up its estimates

of Canada’s population, taking the recent growth rates
from outright contraction to still-slow growth a little below
½%yr. The number of temporary residents in Canada is still
falling, but the revised data do a better job of tracking them,
especially those seeking an extension on their permit. (An alert
When reality gets revised
Luci Ellis
Chief Economist, Westpac Group
for customers who have seen the cross-country population
graph I often use at client events: the Canadian line now looks

different but still shows a sharp slowing.)
There is no fault to be attributed here. Revisions to key
statistics are a part of economic life. Sometimes the underlying
data arrive with a lag, perhaps because of reporting deadlines.
Sometimes the compiling agency improves its methods or
gains access to a better data source. The US and UK examples
above fall into this category. In the Canadian case, better
data and a more accurate definition of scope were involved.

All these revisions should be welcomed. They provide a more
accurate picture of the underlying reality.
Australia, revised
Revisions are a fact of life for Australian statistics, too. I am
still a little scarred by that unexpected –0.5%qtr GDP print
when the September quarter 2016 data were first released on
my first day as RBA Assistant Governor; the figure has since
been revised to 0%qtr and was followed by a big bounce-back
in the December quarter.

The recent revisions mentioned above are less likely to be
replicated in Australia, though. Australia’s CPI is designed such
that the headline (not seasonally adjusted) measure is never
revised. This is because it is used in many legal contracts.
If an error is detected, the ABS makes the adjustment in
subsequent months and quarters rather than revising the
history. Our labour force survey has a much higher response
rate than its UK equivalent – Australians are a compliant lot

when the ABS comes knocking – and measures actual hours
worked more reliably. And our population statistics already
handle the array of temporary resident visas with the Bureau
instead applying a standard 12-month-in-16-month residency
test to count as a resident for the population statistics rather
than using a visa-classification basis.
This last case illustrates Australia’s broader tendency to
favour quality over speed in official statistics. Our CPI and

national accounts data tend to be published a bit later than
peer countries’ data for the same reference period, and our
“Economic data are often revised. Sometimes
the revisions change the story; sometimes
they do not."

### Page 3

the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.
3
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
estimated resident population data is quite a bit lagged
relative to Canada’s (the 12-month-in-16 criteria taking over
a year to confirm). The trade-off is that observers of the
Australian economy must wait longer but can be less worried
about “flash” and “advance” releases of data that can be

quickly revised to tell a completely different story.
Revisions to data still can matter in Australia, and it helps
to be aware of the specific cases where it might occur. For
example, precisely because the population data are very
lagged, the population estimates underlying the initial
releases of the labour force survey and national accounts are
based on preliminary data and get revised later as a matter
of routine. This is why ratios such as the unemployment,

employment-to-population and participation rates provide a
better real-time gauge of labour market conditions than the
monthly change in employment. Similarly, GDP per capita can
be revised even when GDP is not. This type of revision is also
a more likely source of short-term changes in measured hours
worked in the Australian productivity data than the conceptual
change that shifted the UK data.
The usual reporting lags – and occasional correction of

reporting errors by the reporting entity – will always be a factor
in Australia just as it is in other countries. The annual national
accounts, due on 23 October, incorporate additional data, revise
the quarterly estimates and provide first estimates of important
quantities such as the capital stock. Just as importantly, though,
the annual accounts reconcile the different ways of measuring
GDP. In doing so, what we thought we knew about the recent

history of output growth can change.
Every purchase is also a sale, so conceptually, you should get the
same answer for final output regardless of whether you count
how much is produced, how much people spent on it, or how
much income people got for producing it. In practice, though,
the “production”, “expenditure” and “income” approaches do
not always line up until the ABS goes through a more thorough
reconciliation process in compiling the annual accounts.

When the future changes the past
Future data can also change our view of the past. Two good
examples are trend estimation and seasonal adjustment. Future
observations help reveal whether an apparent trend is stable.
As a result, real-time trend estimates can differ considerably
from those calculated with the benefit of later data.
Identifying stable seasonal patterns also requires a reasonable
run of data. Future observations affect whether an earlier

movement is judged to be normal seasonal variation. This
problem is more pronounced for newer series, where there is
not yet enough history to establish what “normal” looks like.
Consider, then, the monthly CPI. This relatively new measure has
only a short run of data with many individual components having
too little history to use standard seasonal adjustment methods.
As Westpac’s senior economist Justin Smirk has previously
highlighted, some components such as footwear are being

‘seasonally adjusted’ by applying Henderson trends, similar
to those used in the ‘trend’ measures of labour market and
national accounts data. For some other components, including
books, furniture, audio-visual and computing equipment and
children’s clothes, certain price falls – usually associated with
sales periods – are just arbitrarily removed from the seasonally
adjusted series. The index is held at its previous month’s level
but otherwise matches the non-seasonally adjusted series.

The seasonally adjusted measures of these CPI components will
inevitably be revised over time, and thus so could the monthly
trimmed mean estimates. This helps explain why the RBA has
said it would, for now, focus on the quarterly trimmed mean
based on the old seasonal factors. That measure will at least
not be buffeted by shifting measurement of seasonality in a
short history. If seasonal patterns are themselves also shifting,
the current underlying trend might look a bit different in a few

years, not just a smoother month-to-month profile.
The broader point here is to remember that economic statistics
are a lens on reality. Sometimes the lens is smudged, sometimes
the scope is off and sometimes what is being read as underlying
trends is wrong. Measures constructed from several imperfect
series – productivity from output and hours worked, or unit labour
costs from productivity and labour costs, or savings rates derived
from income and spending – are especially prone to revision

compared with concepts that can be measured more directly, like
a price. That is a strong argument for humility before making firm
policy pronouncements based on simple data comparisons.

THE WEEK THAT WAS
Cliff Notes: mulling the minutes
consistent with a labour market that is gradually normalising
than one entering a meaningful downturn.
The September FOMC minutes reinforced a distinctly hawkish
message, with participants noting they had "not seen

sufficient progress on lowering inflation in recent months" and
expressing concern that inflation risks remained "skewed to the
upside". Policymakers highlighted that both core services and
core goods inflation remained elevated, with some attributing
persistent goods price strength to the effects of the AI buildout
despite the sector's traditional disinflationary role. There was
also concern that, after more than five years of inflation above
target, elevated inflation could begin to influence inflation

expectations and wage- and price-setting behaviour. In
contrast, labour market conditions were generally viewed as
stable and "close to maximum employment". While a majority
noted the labour market had strengthened somewhat recently,
several participants highlighted unusually low dynamism,
characterised by low hiring, low layoffs, a low job-finding
rate and elevated long-term unemployment. Importantly,
participants generally judged that aggregate wage growth

was moderate and "consistent with inflation moving toward 2
percent", with several explicitly noting that "the labor market
was not currently a source of inflationary pressures".
While participants broadly expected AI-related investment to
boost productivity growth and potential output over time, several
warned that "the AI buildout could cause aggregate demand
to outpace aggregate supply over the medium term, putting
upward pressure on inflation". Consistent with this view, some

participants had raised their estimate of the neutral policy rate
and several judged the current policy stance to be "not restrictive
or only mildly restrictive". Against this backdrop, all participants
supported the September rate increase, and most assessed
that another increase in the federal funds rate would likely be
appropriate by year-end, although future decisions would remain
data dependent.
The September ISM Services PMI released this week painted

a similar picture of an economy that remains resilient but is
gradually losing momentum. The headline index eased to 54.9
from 55.4 in August, with the decline driven by a sharp fall in
business activity and a 9.4pt drop in new export orders, which
slipped into contractionary territory for the first time in eight
The only data release of note in Australia this week was
the October Westpac-MI Consumer Sentiment Survey ,
which reported a 4.7% drop in the headline index. At

80.4, sentiment has tumbled back to deeply pessimistic
levels, similar to the post-pandemic cost-of-living shock
when readings were among the weakest in the survey’s
more than fifty-year history. Indeed, inflation and interest
rate pressures are once again clearly front of mind for
consumers, with both the view on ‘family finances vs a
year ago’ and ‘family finance next 12 months’ falling 8.0%
and 6.4% in the month, respectively. Moreover, the RBA’s

September rate hike looks to have had a significant impact –
sentiment tanked 20% between those surveyed before the
RBA decision and those surveyed afterwards.
Views on the economic outlook were relatively more resilient,
but they still weakened, with the one-year and five-year ahead
sub-indexes falling 1.6% and 0.4% respectively. Consumers
are becoming more ‘on edge’ about job security, albeit not
outright alarmed. The unemployment rate remains relatively

low versus history, although it has drifted up to a cycle high
of 4.6% as labour supply continues to outstrip demand. We
expect it to hold there in next week’s September Labour Force
Survey – see our preview for more detail. All told, with higher
interest rates, renewed inflation pressures and a softening
jobs market all weighing at once, households are entering the
final months of the year on the back foot.
Offshore, the focus was on US data.
Before turning to this week's releases, it is worth noting that

September non-farm payrolls came in softer than expected
across most metrics. Employment rose by only 29k (89k
expected), and there were sizeable downward revisions
to prior months, with a combined 60k removed from July
and August payrolls, leaving gains of just 31k and 29k
respectively. The details pointed to weaker hiring in leisure
& hospitality and education & health services industries,
although construction and manufacturing employment
continued to benefit from ongoing data-centre-related

investment. The unemployment rate edged up to 4.2%, with
softer employment occurring alongside a rise in labour force
participation to 61.8%. Meanwhile wage growth moderated
to 0.1%mth (3.0%yr). Taken together, the report suggests
labour demand is cooling, with the three-month average
pace of job creation slowing to around 50k. However, softer
payroll gains are not necessarily indicative of labour market
deterioration. Lower immigration and ageing demographics

have reduced the pace of employment growth needed to keep
the unemployment rate stable. As such, the report is more
Sian Fenner, Head of Business and Industry Economics
Ryan Wells, Economist
Illiana Jain, Economist

AI investment, productive capacity and
inflation."

months. Labour market conditions improved modestly with
the employment index moving to broadly neutral territory
50.1 after two months below 50, supported by elevated

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
FOCUS ON NEW ZEALAND
Fuel, housing and the bumpy road ahead
Higher fuel costs, a weaker exchange rate, and a softer
housing market have prompted a refresh of our near-term
forecasts. Inflation looks set to finish 2026 above 4%, while
growth could lose a little momentum into year-end. Even so,

we continue to expect the RBNZ to pause in October before
lifting the OCR to 3% in December.
New Zealand’s recovery is still moving forward, but the road
has become rougher. Since our last forecast update in August,
global oil and refined-fuel prices have climbed sharply, the
New Zealand dollar has fallen, and longer-term interest rates
have moved higher. At home, the housing market has remained
weaker than we anticipated. None of those developments,

on their own, are enough to derail the recovery. Together,
however, they point to a tougher finish to 2026 for households
and businesses. We have therefore revisited our near-term
inflation, housing and growth forecasts. The broad story
remains one of continued economic expansion and gradually
improving labour demand, but with a more pronounced
inflation spike and slightly less momentum in consumer
spending. Our OCR call is unchanged: a pause at the October

review, followed by a 25 basis point increase to 3% at the
December Monetary Policy Statement.
A temporary inflation reprieve
We continue to think that consumer prices rose 0.7% in the
September quarter. That would lower annual inflation from
4.1% in June to 3.7% in September, broadly consistent with
the monthly price updates published so far. The final monthly
release for the quarter is due on 16 October and will help firm up
that estimate. But any easing in the annual rate is likely to prove

short-lived. We now expect prices to rise 1.1% in the December
quarter, lifting annual inflation to a three-year high of 4.2% by
year-end. That is half a percentage point above our previous
projection and above the RBNZ’s September forecast of 3.9%.
The reason is easy to see at the petrol pump. Higher global
fuel prices have been amplified by a more than 4% fall in
the New Zealand dollar since the start of September. Local
petrol prices have risen by around 50 cents a litre over the

past month, with the nationwide average price of 91 unleaded
reaching a record $3.55 a litre this week. RBNZ Governor
Breman acknowledged a couple of weeks ago that higher
oil prices have tilted the risks around the near-term inflation
outlook to the upside. Importantly, however, the RBNZ remains
focused on the medium-term inflation outlook rather than
trying to offset every movement in volatile fuel prices.
For now, we have left our quarterly inflation forecasts for 2027

unchanged. Even so, the higher starting point at the end of this
year means annual inflation is likely to remain firm through much
of 2027. Our updated profile has inflation at 3.7% in March
2027, 2.6% in June, 2.5% in September and 1.7% in December.
That is broadly similar to the RBNZ’s path through the first three
quarters, although our forecast falls more quickly late in the
year. We will take a fuller look at the 2027 inflation and growth
outlook next week alongside the monthly Market Outlook.

Housing fails to find its feet
The housing market has also delivered a disappointment. We
had expected prices to stabilise through the second half of
2026, but sales volumes have continued to weaken, and the
median time required to sell a property has lengthened to 48
days. Average prices have kept edging lower, despite firmer
conditions in parts of the South Island with greater exposure
to the strong export economy. The latest indicators do not
suggest that a decisive turn is close. We have therefore revised

our forecast for national house price growth in 2026 from a
0.6% rise to a 1.4% decline. That incorporates quarterly falls
of 0.5% in September and 0.8% in December.
The headwinds are familiar: the labour market remains soft,
mortgage rates are higher and employment growth is still
moderate. Those forces are likely to keep the market subdued
for some time, with risks tilted towards another weak year
in 2027. Changes to the taxation of capital gains on investor

housing following the 2026 General Election may add another
potential drag.
Kelly Eckhold
Chief Economist NZ

### Page 7

the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.
7
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
Regional outcomes will continue to differ. Tourism- and
agriculture-focused areas should benefit from stronger income
and employment growth, supported by robust commodity export
prices. We have lifted our 2026/27 Fonterra Milk Price forecast to
$9.75/kg and set an opening forecast of $9.50/kg for 2027/28. In

contrast, homeowners in the major North Island cities may have to
wait longer for the wealth boost that comes with rising property
values. Data released this week shows that household wealth
continues to be dragged down by declining housing values.
A little less momentum
The weaker housing backdrop matters for the wider economy.
When petrol absorbs more of the household budget and home
values are drifting lower, consumers tend to become more
cautious. That combination is likely to crimp discretionary

spending in the run-up to Christmas and could take some
steam out of the construction recovery. That’s even though our
own card spending data for September  showed resilience in
consumer spending. Recent business and consumer confidence
surveys have nevertheless remained resilient despite the rise
in energy prices and long-term interest rates. Our short-term
growth forecasts therefore remain broadly intact, but we have
trimmed our December quarter consumer spending projection

and now expect GDP growth of 2.5% over 2026. That is still
a solid result, although the balance of risks has shifted to
the downside.
There are several moving parts to watch. Sustained high fuel
prices would squeeze household purchasing power more than
we have allowed for. The sharp lift in long-term interest rates
may also weigh on activity through 2027, even if shorter-
term borrowing costs evolve as expected. The election result
introduces two-sided risks that are difficult to quantify before

the shape and timing of policy changes are known. Against that,
strong agricultural incomes and signs of improving employment
growth should provide a buffer. We have not changed our 2026
labour market forecasts. Hiring appears to be strengthening
gradually, but it will take time before that improvement
translates into a noticeable fall in the unemployment rate.
The RBNZ should still pause before Christmas
Despite the higher near-term inflation track, we are not

changing our OCR forecast. Since the July Monetary Policy
Review, our view has been that the RBNZ would slow the pace
of increases as the OCR approached the Bank’s estimate of a
neutral 3%. The September meeting clearly signalled a pause
in October and one further rise in December. We continue to
regard that as the most likely path. The latest developments
raise headline inflation but, so far, there is little evidence that
inflation expectations are moving higher or that the fuel shock

is generating broader, persistent price pressure. At the same
time, growth risks warrant some caution.
A pause in October would give the Monetary Policy Committee
time to assess the cumulative effect of the increases already
delivered and to refine its strategy for 2027. Financial
conditions have also tightened from an unexpected source:
longer-term interest rates have risen markedly over the past
six weeks. That increase has at least offset the easing impulse

from the lower exchange rate, even though the weaker
currency is adding to imported inflation in the near term. We
still think the RBNZ is likely to have more work to do in 2027,
but there is plenty of data to come before that judgement
needs to be made.
For now, the best description of the outlook is resilient but
less comfortable. Export incomes remain strong, activity is
expanding and the labour market is beginning to improve. But
households face an awkward combination of record petrol

prices, soft home values and higher borrowing costs. That
mix points to a bumpy end to the year: inflation will be hotter,
spending growth a little cooler and the recovery more uneven
across regions. The RBNZ can afford to wait in October, but if
the economy develops broadly as we expect, a further hike to
3% in December will still be needed.

### Page 8

AUS: Sep Labour Force – Employment Change (000s)
Oct 15, Last: 39.5, Westpac f/c: 20 
Market f/c: 20, Range: flat to 35
Employment surprised to the upside in August with a 39k gain. 
Looking through the monthly swings, while momentum has 
improved over recent months, employment growth has largely 
continued to bump around below its historic average and the 
current pace of working age population growth.
Our preview provides a deeper look into the various gauges 
of labour demand – the LFS suggests employment growth 
is sluggish but broader measures in the Q2 Labour Account 
showed a stronger narrative. Meanwhile job ads and business 
surveys are also pulling in different directions.
For September, we have pencilled in a lift in employment of 
+20k, which would see annual growth hold in recent ranges.
AUS: Sep Labour Force – Unemployment Rate (%)
Oct 15, Last: 4.6, Westpac f/c: 4.6 
Market f/c: 4.6, Range: 4.5 to 4.7
The participation rate jumped to 67.1% in August, just under 
the 115-year high recorded in January 2025. With labour 
supply once again outstripping demand, the unemployment 
rate rose from 4.5% to 4.6% – the highest since in late-2021, 
close to rounding up to 4.7% (4.646% at three decimal places).
A softer economy and sub-par employment growth typically 
discourages some people to search for work, but cost-of-living 
pressures and interest rate rises are more than outweighing this 
effect, encouraging more people into the labour market.
With the participation rate expected to pare back to 67.0%, we 
think the unemployment rate will hold steady at 4.6%. Please 
see our preview for more detail.

the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.9
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
NZ: Sep REINZ House Sales and Prices (%ann)
Oct 14, Sales Last: –13.0, Prices Last: –0.9
The housing market has remained soft in recent months,
with sales falling to a two-year low and prices edging lower.
Mortgage rates have risen as the RBNZ has initiated a new

tightening cycle, and a growing supply of unsold homes on the
market means that buyers are under no pressure to bid prices
higher. Conditions remain stronger in the south, with prices
up on last year in Canterbury, Otago and Southland, while
Auckland and Wellington are the weakest markets.
NZ: Sep Retail Card Spending (%mth)
Oct 16, Last: –0.9, Westpac f/c: 0.8
We’re forecasting a 0.8% rise in retail spending in September,
reversing the 0.9% fall seen in the previous month. In part, the

forecast rise in spending is due to the sharp increases in petrol
prices in recent weeks. Those higher fuel costs are weighing on
spending in other categories like hospitality. Nevertheless, we
still expect a 0.5% rise in core (ex-fuel) spending. Stats NZ’s
monthly retail spending figures have been very choppy for the
past two years, and we’re likely to see a bounce following last
month’s unexpected falls in categories like furnishings.
NZ: Sep Selected Consumer Prices (%mth)

Oct 16
This will be the last monthly update ahead of the full quarterly
CPI release on 22 October. We’re currently forecasting a 0.7%
quarterly rise in the CPI (+3.7%yr) and will be watching the
detail of the Selected Prices update to pin down that forecast.
In terms of the big categories, we’re forecasting a roughly 6%
rise in petrol costs, along with a 0.7% rise in food prices. That’s
balanced against continued softness in housing rents (which
are the single largest component of the CPI). The key sources

of risk for our forecast are the volatile travel categories, most
of which recorded sizeable drops last month that we expect to
partially reverse in September.

Document 240: Australia’s Trade in Goods Account, August (2026-10-22)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Australia’s Trade in Goods Account, August (2026-10-22).pdf
Report source: Unknown
Date: 未知日期

### Page 1

#trade #australia
Australia’s Trade in Goods Account, August
By Ryan Wells
Economist, Westpac
13:19 October 01 2026 Share
Another surge in data centre-related equipment imports in August, alongside continued
volatility in gold trade. Trade balance: $0.5bn surplus. Export values: 3.7%mth. Import
values: 5.8%mth.

### Page 2

Australia’s goods trade surplus narrowed to $0.5bn in August from $1.4bn in July (revised down from
$1.9bn previously). This result was significantly lower than expected – the market consensus was for a
broadly stable reading, while Westpac had a slight widening pencilled in. Over the past six months or
so, the trade balance has been oscillating between small deficits and surpluses, largely coinciding with
significant month-to-month volatility in the trade mix. The chief culprit behind this volatility has been

trade in non-monetary gold, among other components.
Before discussing the detail, it is once again worth noting the results for gold up front. In August, non-
monetary gold exports jumped 20%, while imports surged 91%. Excluding gold, the trade figures have
been much more stable, closer to a deficit of $2-3bn per month over the past three months, down from
larger deficits around the start of the year.
Export earnings rose 3.7% in August, bouncing back from a 3.6% decline in July. The trend for exports

so far this year looks a bit more positive, but again, this is tricky to read given the volatility present in
the data. Excluding gold, export earnings were up 1.6% in August. This was largely supported by a
4.7% lift in coal and a 2.8% increase in LNG, with further detail provided by the ABS suggests that an
increase in both volumes and prices contributed. However, iron ore (–1.5%) and rural goods (–1.0%)
weighed on earnings in the month.

### Page 3

The import bill meanwhile increase by 5.8% in August. Once again, a surge in automatic data
processing (ADP) equipment imports – which capture the investment upswing related to data centres –
were in the spotlight, surging 79% in the month up to $4.3bn. There were also an increase in other
capital goods imports centred on telecommunications equipment (+12%) and civil aircraft (+30%) –
large in their own right but dwarfed by the surge in ADP imports in the month.

Our latest estimates have the total pipeline for data centres at around $175bn over the coming decade,
with much of that being front-loaded. It is natural to expect month-to-month volatility in ADP equipment
imports as these large and lumpy data centre projects are worked through, but it is worth highlighting
that August’s jump closely resembles the surge from March, when imports also surged to around
$4.3bn. Given the scale of the increase in August, Q3 is shaping up to be another quarter where data

centre investment will play a significant role in driving growth – while it is true that the data centre build-
out is import-intensive, our modelling suggests that once spending enters Australia, the local economic
impact is likely to be around half of the total investment.

### Page 4

Elsewhere across the import basket, while gold imports also surged, it was effectively offset by a
decline in consumer imports of non-industrial transport equipment (i.e. cars), down 21.2% in August
following the surge from May to July. While still tentative at this stage, it suggests that the momentum in
EV purchases, which played a significant role in the consumer spending story in Q2, may be starting to
wane. Additionally, a further pull-back in fuel imports (–5.0%) in August has extended the downward

trend in recent months, following the initial surge in oil prices in March and April after the war in the
Middle East began. However, with the conflict re-escalating recently and margins on refined fuel
products widening, we could see this trend reverse over the coming months.

IN THIS ISSUE
Macro Strategy—Superintelligence and Superabundance in the Next Decade:
Rapid advances in Artificial Intelligence (AI) have prompted prominent architects of
the new economy to conclude the Singularity is arriving ahead of schedule as signs
of its effects proliferate in the multitude of new AI models running in the increasing

number of data centers driving one of the biggest investment booms in U.S. history.
Superintelligence that surpasses collective human intelligence has long been one of the
predictions of futurists analyzing the implications of the Singularity. Superabundance is
one of the results of Superintelligence predicted by those who have written about the
consequences of this major acceleration in technological innovation. The early arrival of
these radical forces for change poses imminent challenges for society and investors that

will shape the next decade in critical ways.
In particular, the heightened uncertainty about how this new era plays out increases the
utility of a well-diversified approach to wealth management in our view.
Market View—Elevated Debt Supply Is Testing the Market's Appetite: The recent
rise in Treasury yields reflects a confluence of factors, including Federal Reserve (Fed)
policy uncertainty, deteriorating fiscal dynamics, resilient economic growth, persistent

inflation concerns and a record wave of debt issuance from both the U.S. Treasury and
corporate borrowers, particularly in the long end of the curve. Importantly, we view this
as a period of market adjustment rather than a cause for alarm.
Episodes of debt pressures can be a natural consequence of elevated supply, in addition
to other external factors and could be resolved through higher yields that help balance
supply and demand. While interest rate volatility may remain elevated and long-term

yields could settle at structurally higher levels, today's combination of attractive nominal
and real yields presents a potential opportunity for investors to extend beyond cash and
short-duration holdings and move closer to strategic duration targets.

Officer (CIO).
